Env **NAMES** only: `KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`, `LEAD_WEBHOOK_URL`. **Zero new dependencies** — both adapters speak REST over the platform `fetch`; no `@vercel/kv`, no `@upstash/redis`, no `pg`. | Precedence (first configured wins) is simpler, but the defect being fixed is *"the lead exists in one place and that place failed"*. **An operator who configures two sinks asked for two traces** — and the family's readable copy is a spreadsheet, not a Redis console. | **Three lines in `index.ts`** (return the first non-null sink) + `composite.ts` and its tests to `_legacy/`. **Zero call-site changes** — `resolveLeadSink` returns `LeadSink` either way and the route cannot tell the difference. |
| **D-60** | **The webhook payload is a CONTRACT WITH A HUMAN'S SPREADSHEET: nine flat string fields, always all nine, always this order** — `id · receivedAt · source · locale · name · phone · email · organization · message`. Optional fields arrive as `""`, **never `null`, never missing**, so columns never shift. `id` is the ULID, so arrival order sorts naturally and correlates with the KV key and the notification. **Nothing else is sent** — no secret, no IP, no user agent, no field the visitor did not provide. `deliveryState` is deliberately **absent**: it is always `pending` at write time, and a column that never updates would be a fact this sink cannot observe. | A nested or variable-shaped payload is easier to emit and impossible to put in a sheet. | Append-only: **fields may only ever be added; a rename or reorder is a breaking change to someone's spreadsheet.** Enforced, not merely documented — the key set **and its order** are asserted with `toEqual`, so drift goes red before a sheet does. | ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED IN PART BY D-118 (W9-FIX3) — kept unedited, per this ledger's rule that a record which edits away its own history stops being one.** The payload is now **TEN** fields: `deliveryState` was appended at the right-hand end. **The reasoning quoted above was sound for ONE write and wrong about the pipeline — because it was never one write.** `persistDeliveryState` re-`store()`s the settled record, and on a keyless sink that is a **second HTTP POST that was measured byte-identical to the first** (D-110). The column that "never updates" was in fact the only thing the second row had to say.
| **D-61** | **DEFERRED — partial failure is invisible to the caller.** If KV stores and the webhook is down, the route sees plain success; the only trace is one `console.warn` (sink name, kind, lead id, detail — **no URL, no token, no payload**, asserted). The port's success arm is `{stored:true, id}` with **no room for "…but one sink was down"** — and that arm was deliberately kept minimal by W9-A so that `result.id` could never disagree with a carried record. | Widening the success arm now would reopen the very shape W9-A compile-proved shut, mid-wave, with two delegates coding against it. | **Closer: a richer success arm in `port.ts` (e.g. a `degraded` list) or a route-supplied observer.** ⚠️ **Honest consequence to state in the handoff: with both sinks configured, an operator can believe they have two traces while silently having one.** The `console.warn` is the only signal, and nobody reads server logs on a memorial site. |
| **D-62** | **502 IS RETIRED ENTIRELY — a notify failure can no longer change the status code, because the lead is already saved.** W9-C found the two halves of its own brief contradicting each other: *"provider rejects → 502 must not regress"* and *"a notify failure must not change the status"* cannot both hold, since 502 **is** a notify failure changing the status. **It implemented the wave's intent (201) and reported the contradiction rather than picking one silently.** New rule: once the sink says `stored:true`, the visitor is told the truth — **201 `{ok, id}`** — and a rejected, unreachable or unconfigured mail provider only writes `deliveryState: 'failed'` onto the stored record. Mail-unconfigured, which used to be a 5xx, is now also 201. | The old 502 told the visitor "we failed" about an enquiry **that was sitting safely in storage**, inviting them to re-send a lead the family already had. Keeping it would have preserved a red test at the cost of lying to the one person the site exists for. | **Reversing means re-teaching the route that email is the record** — the exact inversion this wave removed. The pre-notify status decision is one `const response = created(result.id)` above the try; a reverser must move it back down and re-widen the failure arm. ⚠️ **The seat wrote the contradiction. A delegate caught it. That is the third false premise of the night traced to this seat's own briefs** (see D-58, and the `locale` premise W9-A refused). |
| **D-63** | **STORAGE FAILURE IS THREE DIFFERENT ANSWERS, because the kinds mean genuinely different things to a retrying client.** `not_configured` → **503 `delivery_unavailable`** (nothing is wired; retrying now is pointless but the site is not broken). `transient` → **503 `storage_unavailable` + `Retry-After: 30`** (the only case where a retry can work, so it is the only case that invites one). `permanent` → **500 `storage_failed`** (the request itself will never succeed; do not retry). **In every one of the three, nothing is stored and nothing is claimed** — no id is minted, no success shape is returned. | Collapsing all three to one 500 is fewer branches and was the shape before this wave. But `Retry-After` is only honest on the transient arm, and a blanket 500 either invites retries that cannot help or suppresses the one that can. | **Cheap and local** — the three arms are one `switch` over `failure.kind` in `app/api/lead/route.ts`, each asserted by its own test. Merging them is deleting branches; splitting them again means re-deriving which kind deserves the header. |
| **D-64** | **THE HONEYPOT DECOY RETURNS 201 `{ok:true}` WITH NO ID — the one place W9-C deliberately deviated from its brief's literal wording, and it was right to.** The brief said the trap should "look exactly like success to the bot." A full success shape carries an **id**, and an id is evidence of a stored record. **Minting one for a lead that was never written would be a fabricated identifier in a 2xx — precisely the class of lie this rebuild exists to remove, and a direct Law 8 violation.** So the decoy is success-shaped in **status**, empty in **evidence**: 201, `{ok:true}`, no id, **zero sink calls (asserted by call count, not by status)**. The guard runs **before** validation, so a trapped bot also learns nothing about the schema. | A bot that only reads the status cannot tell. A bot that diffs response bodies can. **The invariant outranks the deception** — and the alternative was a route that returns identifiers for things that do not exist. | **One line** to mint a fake id, and the site loses the property that *an id in a 2xx means a stored record*. ⚠️ **That property is load-bearing for every other decision in this wave** — D-62 rests on it entirely. Reversing it is not a tweak to the honeypot; it is a retraction of the wave. |
| **D-65** | **NO `waitUntil`, NO `after()`, NO FLOATING PROMISE — notify is `await`ed before the response is RETURNED, but after it is DECIDED.** `const response = created(result.id)` is constructed **above** the try, so no notify outcome can reach back and alter the status (this is the mechanism that makes D-62 structural rather than merely intended). Work scheduled after a serverless response may be killed mid-flight; W9-C declined to schedule any: *"I will not claim durability I cannot demonstrate."* | Backgrounding the email would shave its latency off the visitor's wait. It would also mean the `deliveryState` write races a dying process — the site would be *faster at not knowing whether it emailed anyone.* | **⚠️ HONEST LIMIT, and it is a real one: a process death between the sink write and the notify write leaves `deliveryState: 'pending'` forever, and NO SWEEPER EXISTS.** The lead is safe and readable — only the delivery flag is stranded. **Closer: a reconciliation pass over records older than N minutes still `pending`. Not built, not scheduled, and it needs a storage that can be listed** — which today means KV, not the webhook. Logged to §DEFERRED. |
| **D-66** | **THE PER-IP RATE LIMIT FAILS OPEN, ON PURPOSE.** 5 requests / 60 s / IP → **429 + `Retry-After`**; but when **no address header is present at all**, the request is **allowed**, not bucketed. The alternative — treating "unknown IP" as one shared bucket — would collapse every header-less visitor into a single counter, so **the sixth genuine enquiry of the hour is refused because five strangers came first.** Companion guards: idempotency (`Idempotency-Key`, else an exact fingerprint over `[ip,name,phone,email,organization,message,locale]`, 10 min / 200 entries) stores **once** and replays the **same id**; the payload cap is checked on `Content-Length` **before** `request.text()` *and* on the raw string before parse. | Fail-closed is the stricter posture and the usual reflex. On a memorial site the cost of a false positive is not an annoyed user — **it is a bereaved family never learning someone wanted to book.** A spammer who can strip headers can also rotate IPs; the limiter was never the thing stopping them. | **One boolean** in the limiter's no-address branch. ⚠️ **Both the limiter and the idempotency cache are IN-PROCESS MEMORY**: they reset on cold start and do not coordinate across instances, so on serverless the effective limit is *per instance*, not per IP. Stated here because a limit that is believed global and is actually per-instance is exactly the kind of name-not-a-thing this ledger exists to catch. |
| **D-67** | **"FAILED" IS REFUSED AS A COUNTER — the funnel names two different disasters and never adds them.** W10-C would not ship a single `failed` number, because **`failed: 7` cannot tell a bereaved family whether seven people are waiting for a call or seven people are gone.** Instead: **`storedButNobodyTold`** (the enquiry is safe and readable; only the notification failed — recoverable) and **`neverStored`** (the enquiry does not exist — unrecoverable). They are separately derived, differently named, and **no expression anywhere in the module sums them.** The three storage-failure arms feed only the second. Invariant that falls out and is asserted: `attempts = stored + neverStored + refusedByForm`. | One `failed` counter is the conventional funnel shape and half the code. It is also the shape that lets the worst outcome hide inside the tolerable one. | **Reversing means re-merging two names into one and deleting the invariant** — i.e. deliberately destroying the distinction between "someone is waiting for a call" and "someone is lost". ⚠️ Nothing prevents it in code; **this ledger row is the guard.** |
| **D-68** | **THE COUNTERS ARE DURABLE-ONLY, WITH NO IN-MEMORY FALLBACK — and an unconfigured store returns a shape with NO `totals` FIELD AT ALL.** Serverless memory resets on cold start and does not coordinate across instances, so an in-memory counter would **silently under-report, possibly to zero.** W10-C refused the fallback on the grounds that **it would substitute a lying number for a missing one.** Unconfigured → `{measured:false, missing:[ENV NAMES]}`, so *"nobody enquired"* and *"nobody was counting"* **cannot be confused by a caller that type-checks.** ⛔ **Never a fabricated zero.** Uses the **same** KV store, transport and REST protocol as the leads (`KV_REST_API_URL`/`KV_REST_API_TOKEN`) — **no new dependency, no second timeout.** Key prefix is `lead_funnel:` not `lead:funnel:` so counters never surface in an operator's `SCAN 0 MATCH lead:*`. | An in-memory counter works today with zero configuration — and would quietly become wrong the first time the platform scaled or slept. | Cheap to add a fallback later; **the reason not to is recorded here, not in code.** ⚠️ Consequence to state in the handoff: **with no KV attached, the funnel measures NOTHING — not zero, nothing.** |
| **D-69** | **WHAT COUNTS AS AN ENQUIRY — bots, refusals and double-clicks are tallied but are NOT attempts.** Honeypot hits → `discardedBots` (**not** an attempt: *"counting bots would tell this family their brother's lectures are in demand when they are not"* — but kept **visible**, because the route's own honest limit concedes the trap can catch a real person, and **a trap that can catch humans must at minimum be countable**). 429/413 → `refusedBeforeReading` (never reached validation; a flood of 4,000 would otherwise read as 4,000 interested schools). Idempotent replay → `duplicates` (**one** enquiry; a fact about double-clicks, not interest). **422 IS an attempt** — a person filled the form in and the form refused them — **split by language, because a Hebrew-only failure rate is a bug in the form.** | The easy shape is "every request is an attempt". On a memorial site that inflates a grieving family's sense of demand with bot traffic. | Per-outcome and local: one `OUTCOME_MEANING` table maps each of the 11 outcomes to `{attempt, stored, told}`, and **every total is folded from it at read time — a total is never written, so it cannot drift from its parts.** |

The one call that decides whether this enquiry exists"*) is **not**. A throwing adapter there rejects `POST` → opaque framework 500 → **lead not stored, not counted, nobody told** — the exact total loss W9 exists to prevent, arriving through the one path nobody guarded. **Corroboration: the suite declares fake-sink behaviour `{kind:'throw'}` at `route.test.ts:116` and handles it at 134, and NO TEST EVER USES IT** — the coverage was intended and never landed. ⚠️ **W10-C reported it and refused to fix it** (outside its write-set; fixing it meant restructuring a proven file). **The seat verified both claims directly before spending a delegate on them.** | This is the **fourth** time tonight a "finished and proven" artefact was found wanting **by someone reading it for an unrelated reason** — see D-55, D-56, D-58. **The pattern is now the most reliable finding of the run: defects are caught by the next person who reads the file, not by the tests the author wrote.** | **Handed to atomic fix delegate W9-FIX1**, which must choose ONE level and defend it: guard the call site, or **make `resolveLeadSink`'s documented totality true by construction** so the unguarded `await` is correct by design rather than by luck. ⛔ **Not both — a guard at two levels is a patch over a patch and leaves the path untestable at either.** Re-verified by the W9 gate, which wrote none of it. |

An inline style has no stylesheet to fail to load, **so hiding became MEASURED** (`getComputedStyle(wrapper).display === 'none'`) instead of NOT-MEASURED. It also beats off-screen positioning on a second axis: **browser autofill skips a control computed as not displayed, but will fill one merely parked off-screen** — and an autofilled honeypot discards a real enquiry while showing the visitor success. The poster IS the family's choice of frame.** **(J3)** A press mention requires **outlet AND date AND url** — it is a verifiability claim, not prose, and any two without the third is uncheckable by a booker in the thirty seconds they will give it. | Each alternative is the conventional, permissive one, and each would have let a half-true state ship looking finished. | **J2 and J3 are the expensive ones to reverse** — they make the tempting fabrications in D-78 literally unconstructible, and loosening either re-opens *"we have the link, just ship it."* |

ZERO JSX changed**, so the idle render is byte-identical **by construction**. ⛔ **It refused to import `parseLeadId`/`LEAD_ID_LENGTH` into the client component, and the reasoning is the better half of the fix:** `parseLeadId` returns a **branded `LeadId`** meaning *"this identifies a record in our store"* — ⚠️ **minting that brand on the client out of an untrusted network body would launder a stranger-controlled string into the one type the repo uses to mean the opposite.** And a shape check would be **wrong in a way nothing could see**: if the route moved ULID→uuid, every honest success would become a failure, and **the client suite mocks the boundary while the route suite never renders this component** — the `bg-gold` shape at the client/server seam. **So it checks presence and non-blankness: "this component can tell evidence from no evidence, never true evidence from forged evidence."** | ⚠️ **Decided, not defaulted: absent, blank, non-string, nested-elsewhere, array body, bare-string body, unparseable body and no-body are ALL failure; '', label, name, phone, email, organization, message, locale]`. ⚠️ **The `Idempotency-Key` header is ONE PART OF THE TUPLE, never the whole key — so it can only ever SPLIT a namespace further; it can no longer merge two submissions that differ in address or in one typed character.** That also kills the old `k:`/`f:` duality: one fact, one place. **On the address-less case the brief warned about:** those visitors do share the `''` namespace, **but the payload is in the key unconditionally**, so two of them collapse only when their enquiries are **byte-identical in every field** — the pre-existing, documented fingerprint trade, not a route to somebody else's id. ⛔ **It considered and REJECTED a per-request nonce for `null`: that would switch the duplicate guard off entirely for every visitor behind a non-forwarding proxy, and the double-click is the case the cache exists for.** | ✅ **The race is closed BY CONSTRUCTION: the map now holds the store's own `Promise<LeadStoreResult>`, never an id**, reserved **synchronously with zero awaits between the check and the reservation.** A concurrent caller **waits** and is told exactly what the owning store reported. ⚠️ **Why it can never hand out a phantom id: the reservation contains no id. ## §DEFERRED — with what would close each

| Item | Why deferred | What would close it |
|---|---|---|
| **~30 headers across this repo claim a COMPILE refusal with nothing that re-executes it (D-119).** Executing assertions exist in only three directories; `lib/leads/sinks/**`, `app/api/lead/**`, `i18n/**`, `lib/seo/**` and seven `components/sections/*` files claim refusals backed by prose alone. | The claims are believed TRUE today — this is a **proof gap, not a defect** — and W14 (independent verify) plus W15 (handoff) are the morning deliverable. Spending the remaining delegates here would trade the night's actual product for hardening a guarantee that currently holds. | **Closer: apply the `lib/leads/__tests__/port-structural.test.ts` pattern per directory — one atomic delegate each, mechanical, and now proven twice** (`pii-structural.test.ts`, `port-structural.test.ts`). ⚠️ **Write every probe with an inert placeholder first, compile, read the REAL error, then convert in place — and add positive controls, or a broken import makes every negative vacuously green.** |
| **`/en/<missing>` renders HEBREW (D-162, D-177).** Landing Mati's Hebrew 404 copy diverged the catalogues correctly; the `[locale]` 404 boundary cannot read the request locale without destroying the prerender of `/he` and `/en` (D-176 measured: `he.html` and `en.html` vanish while the route table still prints `●`). | Option (a) request-read is forbidden by measurement; (b) client-side selection puts JS on the two content pages, leaves the served bytes Hebrew anyway, and breaks D-137; (c) the structural fix needs an `experimental` flag in `next.config.js` plus retiring `app/layout.tsx` — a whole-site routing change refused on measured risk with three siblings live in the repo. **Non-convergence rule reached.** | **Closer, in order: (i) `experimental.rootParams: true` in `next.config.js` · (ii) `app/layout.tsx` → `_legacy/` so `app/[locale]/layout.tsx` becomes the root layout · (iii) read the locale from `next/root-params` · (iv) a BUILD-ARTEFACT gate asserting `he.html` and `en.html` exist, because per D-176 the route table will not say.** ⚠️ **Reversal cost of what landed tonight: one describe block and one comment — zero runtime lines.** |
| **Guard-0 refusals (403 foreign origin, 415 wrong media type) are NOT COUNTED in the funnel (D-171).** `lib/leads/log.ts` owns the outcome vocabulary and none of its eleven outcomes describes a forged cross-site POST honestly. **A sustained cross-origin flood is invisible on the funnel page and shows only in 429s and the access log.** | W16-C refused to mis-tally them into `refusedUnparseable` (*"(400). Not a submission"*), which operators read as broken clients; and `lib/leads/**` was another delegate's write-set, with a sibling building a read path in it concurrently. | **Closer: add a `refusedForeign` outcome to `lib/leads/log.ts`'s `OUTCOME_MEANING` table and one `count()` call at Guard 0 in `app/api/lead/route.ts`.** ⚠️ **Both files are now free. One atomic delegate; the read path built this run (`readLeadFunnel` → the admin route) will surface it with no further work.** |
| **A lead can be stranded at `deliveryState: 'pending'` forever — no sweeper exists (D-65).** If the process dies between the sink write and the delivery-state write, the enquiry itself is safe and readable; only the flag saying whether anyone emailed the family is stuck. **Nothing scans for these.** | Building a reconciliation pass mid-wave needs a storage that can be **listed** — which today means KV, not the webhook — and would have widened W9 into a scheduling problem while two delegates were coding against the port. | **Closer: a pass over records older than N minutes still `pending`, re-notifying or flagging them.** Needs (a) a listable sink and (b) somewhere to run it on a schedule. ⚠️ Until then `pending` means **"unknown"**, not "in progress" — and the ledger must not let it read as the latter. |
| **Partial sink failure is invisible to the caller (D-61).** With both sinks configured and one down, the route sees plain success; the only trace is one `console.warn`. | The port's success arm was deliberately compile-shut by W9-A so `result.id` could never disagree with a carried record; widening it mid-wave would reopen exactly that shape. | **Closer: a `degraded` list on the success arm of `port.ts`, or a route-supplied observer.** ⚠️ **An operator can believe they have two traces while silently having one**, and nobody reads server logs on a memorial site. |
| **The rate limiter and the idempotency cache are per-process memory, not per-IP globally (D-66).** On serverless they reset on cold start and do not coordinate across instances. | In-process is correct for the single-instance case the site actually runs today, and a shared store would have added the very storage dependency W9-B kept at zero new packages. | **Closer: move both buckets behind the same KV sink the leads use** — the credentials are already seeded (`KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`). Until then, **"5 per minute per IP" is per instance**; state it that way or not at all. |
| **Comment churn: the cookie-gating fact is documented in TWO files** (`i18n/routing.ts` and `middleware.ts`). Three delegates edited comments about it in sequence (W3-I flagged, W3-K fixed one side, W3-L fixed the other), and a cross-reference may now be stale. **This is the patch-over-a-patch spiral in comment form**, and the root cause is ONE FACT TWO PLACES, not any individual edit. | **Closer: give the fact ONE owner — `i18n/routing.ts`, which owns the config — and have `middleware.ts` point at it rather than restate it.** Comment-only, 2 files. Not done tonight: it is documentation, it blocks nothing, and a fourth comment edit before the structural fix would be the very spiral it describes. |
